1138	<step name="handle_partial_wave_execution">
1139	If `WAVE_FILTER` was used, re-run plan discovery after execution:
1140	
1141	```bash
1142	POST_PLAN_INDEX=$(gsd_run query phase-plan-index "${PHASE_NUMBER}")
1143	```
1144	
1145	Apply the same "incomplete" filtering rules as earlier:
1146	- ignore plans with `has_summary: true`
1147	- if `--gaps-only`, only consider `gap_closure: true` plans
1148	
1149	**If incomplete plans still remain anywhere in the phase:**
1150	- STOP here
1151	- Do NOT run phase verification
1152	- Do NOT mark the phase complete in ROADMAP/STATE
1153	- Present:
1154	
1155	```markdown
1156	## Wave {WAVE_FILTER} Complete
1157	
1158	Selected wave finished successfully. This phase still has incomplete plans, so phase-level verification and completion were intentionally skipped.
1159	
1160	/gsd-execute-phase {phase} ${GSD_WS}                # Continue remaining waves
1161	/gsd-execute-phase {phase} --wave {next} ${GSD_WS}  # Run the next wave explicitly
1162	```
1163	
1164	**If no incomplete plans remain after the selected wave finishes:**
1165	- continue with the normal phase-level verification and completion flow below
1166	- this means the selected wave happened to be the last remaining work in the phase
1167	</step>
1168	
1169	<step name="code_review_gate" required="true">
1170	**This step is REQUIRED to evaluate the capability hook.** When the code-review capability is active, auto-invoke code review on the phase's source changes. Advisory only — never blocks execution flow. Also dispatches advisory execute:post gate hooks (e.g. tdd.review-checkpoint).
1171	
1172	**Capability gate:**
1173	```bash
1174	EXECUTE_POST_HOOKS_JSON=${EXECUTE_POST_HOOKS_JSON:-$(gsd_run loop render-hooks execute:post --raw)}
1175	```
1176	
1177	Resolve active step hooks from `EXECUTE_POST_HOOKS_JSON` where `kind == "step"` and `ref.skill == "code-review"`.
1178	
1179	If no active code-review step hook exists: display "Code review skipped (code-review capability inactive)" and proceed to gate dispatch.
1180	
1181	**Invoke review:**
1182	```
1183	Skill(skill="gsd-${ref.skill}", args="${PHASE_NUMBER}")
1184	```
1185	
1186	**Check results using deterministic path (not glob):**
1187	```bash
1188	PADDED=$(printf "%02d" "${PHASE_NUMBER}")
1189	REVIEW_FILE="${PHASE_DIR}/${PADDED}-REVIEW.md"
1190	REVIEW_STATUS=$(sed -n '/^---$/,/^---$/p' "$REVIEW_FILE" | grep "^status:" | head -1 | cut -d: -f2 | tr -d ' ')
1191	```
1192	
1193	If REVIEW_STATUS is not "clean" and not "skipped" and not empty, display:
1194	```
1195	Code review found issues. Consider running:
1196	/gsd-code-review ${PHASE_NUMBER} --fix
1197	```
1198	
1199	**Error handling:** If the Skill invocation fails or throws, catch the error, display "Code review encountered an error (non-blocking): {error}" and proceed to gate dispatch. Review failures must never block execution.
1200	
1201	**Execute:post gate hook dispatch.** After code review, dispatch all active gate hooks from `EXECUTE_POST_HOOKS_JSON` where `kind == "gate"`:
1202	
1203	For each active gate hook:
1204	```bash
1205	GATE_RESULT=$(gsd_run check ${hook.check.query} "${PHASE_NUMBER}" --raw)
1206	CHECK_EXIT=$?
1207	```
1208	
1209	**Gate evaluation** uses the same two-step contract as `execute:wave:post` above: **Step 1** — if the check command failed (non-zero `CHECK_EXIT`, empty/unparseable output), `onError == "halt"` stops and surfaces the error, `onError == "skip"` warns and continues to the next hook (do not read `block`). **Step 2** (command succeeded) — a blocking gate (`hook.blocking == true`) halts on `GATE_RESULT.block == true` with its message/table (never bypassed by `onError`); an advisory gate (`hook.blocking == false`) shows its `table`/summary when `block == true` or `message` is non-empty, then continues; a blocking gate with `block == false` continues silently.
1210	
1211	**TDD review escalation (overrides the advisory default for the `tdd.review-checkpoint` gate only).** The tdd `execute:post` gate is declared `blocking: false`, so by the generic contract above it displays its `message`/table and continues. There is ONE documented exception (see `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/.claude/gsd-core/references/execute-mvp-tdd.md`): when `MVP_MODE=true` AND `TDD_MODE=true` AND `GATE_RESULT.block == true` (one or more TDD plans miss a RED or GREEN gate commit), the end-of-phase TDD review escalates from advisory to **blocking under MVP+TDD** — refuse to mark the phase complete and present:
1212	
1213	```
1214	Phase blocked: {N} TDD plan(s) violate the RED→GREEN gate sequence under MVP+TDD.
1215	Resolve and re-run /gsd execute-phase, or override with /gsd execute-phase {phase} --force-mvp-gate to ship anyway.
1216	```
1217	
1218	(`--force-mvp-gate` is the documented, not-yet-implemented escape hatch.) Outside MVP+TDD, TDD-review violations remain advisory (table shown, execution continues).
1219	
1220	**Proceed rule:** If `MVP_MODE && TDD_MODE && GATE_RESULT.block == true` for `tdd.review-checkpoint`: STOP — do NOT proceed to `close_parent_artifacts`, `regression_gate`, `verify_phase_goal`, or `phase.complete`. Otherwise proceed normally.
1221	</step>
1222	
1223	<step name="close_parent_artifacts">
1224	**For decimal/polish phases only (X.Y pattern):** Close the feedback loop by resolving parent UAT and debug artifacts.
1225	
1226	**Skip if** phase number has no decimal (e.g., `3`, `04`) — only applies to gap-closure phases like `4.1`, `03.1`.

220	## Accumulated Context
221	
222	### Key Decisions
223	
224	See PROJECT.md Key Decisions table for full list with outcomes.
225	
226	Recent for v1.1:
227	
228	- make_temp_queue in crates/sdk/src/queue.rs uses pid+counter (not tid+counter) to prevent inter-run temp dir collisions (Phase 45 P01 Rule-1 fix)
229	- T-45-07 uses root-shortcut path (folder_ipns_name==root_ipns_name) for deterministic resolve_folder_key test without network; marked for #15 extension
230	- T-45-08 placed in crates/fuse/src/lib.rs (not apps/desktop) to keep characterization tests co-located with merge_folder_children under test
231	- Network-first with self-hosted Someguy + DB fallback adopted as IPNS resolution strategy (revised from DB-first during Phase 19 context -- see 19-SCOPING_RATIONALE.md #1)
232	- rootFolderKey DB copy kept as permanent fallback (never drop column, IPFS copy for recovery independence)
233	- BYO-IPFS affects pinning only, all IPNS publishes still route through CipherBox API
234	- PERF requirements split across Phase 18 (server-side, pre-change) and Phase 22 (client + load testing, post-change)
235	- IPFS/IPNS histogram buckets: 1ms-30s exponential (14 buckets); republish batch: 1s-120s (10 buckets)
236	- Source label (db/network) only for resolve operations; empty string for pin/cat/publish
237	- Alloy scrapes Kubo directly via Docker internal network (ipfs:5001), not proxied through API
238	- Kubo Health dashboard panels use fallback Go runtime metrics alongside libp2p metrics pending post-deploy verification
239	- IPNS-specific histograms: resolve 50ms-30s, publish 100ms-60s with source/outcome labels
240	- Null resolve results (not found) excluded from IPNS histogram observations
241	- Used axios-functions orval client for @cipherbox/api-client (plain functions, no React deps)
242	- sdk-core IPFS ops use direct axios/fetch (not api-client) for upload progress; IPNS ops use api-client generated functions
243	- Bin/share operations take explicit context objects (BinOperationContext, ShareOperationContext) instead of Zustand stores
244	- Share module accepts callback functions for API calls to stay transport-decoupled
245	- Moved @cipherbox/core from dependencies to devDependencies in crypto (test-only cross-package assertions)
246	- Kubo pebbleds datastore (LSM-tree) configured via IPFS_PROFILE=server,pebbleds; requires fresh volume on deploy
247	- [Phase 56 P02] publish_with_cas_retry uses sync Fn(u64) closure seam; folder site keeps its own CAS loop (async merge-on-conflict path cannot delegate to sync helper)
248	- [Phase 56 P02] D-01a: per-file/bin publish Conflict exhaustion returns Err→EIO; journal-on-exhaustion deferred (no JournalOp::FilePublish/BinPublish variant)
249	- [Phase 56 P02] D-11: matched_by_stable_id=false clears children_loaded and children to force fresh subtree load on display-name-only fallback match
250	- SDK concurrent pins require pebbleds datastore (synergistic); concurrent pins alone cause regression at 50 clients
251	- Combined per-task commits into single commit due to pre-commit hook requiring api-client regeneration with entity/dto/controller changes
252	- Desktop root folder detected by inode::ROOT_INO at publish call sites (simpler than modifying build_folder_metadata return type)
253	- Desktop initialize_vault produces v2 blob for new users from day one (not just on migration)
254	- decrypt_metadata_from_ipfs_public transparently handles both v1 JSON and v2 binary blobs
255	- VaultExportDto returns only rootIpnsName and derivationMethod (crypto columns dropped)
256	- Recovery tool IPNS resolution uses gateway /ipns/ HEAD request with redirect following (most reliable without API dependency)
257	- fetchAndDecryptMetadata handles both v1 JSON and v2 binary blobs transparently for folder sync
258	- Zero-crypto vault schema: server stores only ownerPublicKey and rootIpnsName, all crypto material lives exclusively in IPFS v2 blobs
259	- DB crypto columns (encrypted_root_folder_key, encrypted_root_ipns_private_key, migrated_at) fully dropped -- no fallback paths
260	- PinningProvider interface: KuboProvider uses Basic auth, PsaProvider uses Bearer auth, matching each protocol's native auth model
261	- PsaProvider.pin() throws intentionally; pinByCid() is the correct PSA workflow (CID-reference-only protocol)
262	- Connection test uses sequential probe: Kubo /api/v0/id first, then PSA /pins, with 10s timeout per probe
263	- CID registration gated to BYO users only via ForbiddenException (non-BYO users cannot bypass upload relay)
264	- Advisory quota: checkQuota() always true for BYO, getQuota() includes advisory boolean flag for UI display
265	- pinFn injection pattern: optional pinFn parameter on sdkCore.uploadFile() replaces addToIpfs when BYO mode active
266	- External+Kubo bypasses CipherBox entirely; external+PSA uses relay for CID only; dual does both with best-effort secondary
267	- PsaProvider.pinByCid() accessed via cast in client.ts (PSA-specific, not on PinningProvider interface)
268	- Migration uses existing BullMQ pattern with pin-migration queue name; TEE decrypts ECIES-encrypted provider configs in-enclave with epoch key
269	- SSRF protection on TEE migration: validates URL structure (HTTPS-only, no private IPs) and DNS resolution (rebinding check)
270	- BYO config stored as encrypted IPNS entry using rootFolderKey -- no server-side credential storage (zero-knowledge preserved)
271	- Dedicated IPNS key derived via HKDF with context string byo-ipfs-config from vault keypair
272	- BYO benchmark execution (21-07 Task 4) deferred -- requires external IPFS provider infrastructure; test scenarios ready to run when provider available
273	- BYO config loaded at login via IPNS resolve with graceful fallback to cipherbox-only mode
274	- Source unpin is best-effort and non-fatal after verified CID transfer to destination
275	- Cargo workspace with centralized deps at repo root; cipherbox-crypto crate as foundation for all Rust SDK extraction
276	- Module re-export pattern in desktop crypto/mod.rs preserves all existing crate::crypto::* paths without touching call sites
277	- cipherbox-core crate layered on cipherbox-crypto: folder, file, bin, vault_blob, ipns, registry, decrypt, error modules
278	- File module re-exports FileMetadata types from folder.rs (shared AES encryption context with parent folder key)
279	- decrypt module moved from fuse to crypto re-export (domain logic, not FUSE-specific)
280	- Hand-structured API client crate rather than openapi-generator (modest API surface, proven code, no Java/Docker CI dependency)
281	- critical-section std feature required for standalone ecies linking (Tauri provides it in desktop builds)
282	- Shared test vectors in tests/vectors/ JSON files loadable by both Rust and TypeScript for CI parity gates
283	- SyncDaemon uses Arc<dyn Fn(SyncStatus)> generic callback instead of Tauri AppHandle for testability
284	- Desktop api/client.rs re-exports cipherbox_api_client::ApiClient as type alias to unify types across modules
285	- Desktop AppState wraps Arc<KeyState> from SDK; all key material accessed via state.sdk.*
286	- Keychain operations kept as desktop-specific keychain.rs module (not in api-client crate)
287	- Desktop api/ and crypto/ directories fully removed; all imports use workspace crates directly
288	- CI parity gate uses needs.changes.outputs.src (not nonexistent packages) for trigger condition
289	- Desktop-e2e binary paths updated to target/debug/ to match workspace cargo build output
290	- PinataProvider uses dual base URLs: uploads.pinata.cloud (fixed) for upload, api.pinata.cloud (configurable) for management
291	- pinWithMode treats Pinata like Kubo: direct upload bypasses CipherBox relay entirely
292	- Connection test probe order updated: Kubo -> Pinata -> PSA; pinata.cloud URLs skip Kubo probe
293	- BYO Pinata baselines: pin p50=2.0s (+47% vs local Kubo), tail latency p99 13.5% better, 98% CipherBox API load reduction per file
294	- perf.ts PERF_ENABLED evaluated once at module load (zero overhead in production); **CIPHERBOX_PERF** global for opt-in production debugging
295	- Load test thresholds set at 2-3x observed baselines; spike test most generous (15s/15%); vitest expect() for CI failure on breach
296	- Write-share authorization in upsertFolderIpns falls through to create-new-entry when no write share found (preserves backward compat for owner first publish)
297	- TEE enrollFolder uses existing.userId (FolderIpns owner) for write-share publishes, not authenticated userId
298	- Per-file IPNS records created for shared uploads (same as owner uploads) instead of empty fileMetaIpnsName PoC shortcut
299	- File IPNS private key dual-wrapped: owner key in FilePointer, recipient key in share_keys (keyType: file-ipns)
300	- addShareKeys API relaxed to allow write-share recipients to add keys to their own share
301	- TextEditorDialog has separate shared file save path via onSaveSharedFile callback
302	- Shared file download/view falls back to fileKeyEncrypted from metadata when no share_key exists
303	- FilePointer resolution uses FileMetadata directly (no separate ResolvedFileMetadata struct)
304	- FilePointer resolution scoped to parent folder via get_unresolved_file_pointers_for_parent() to avoid wrong-folder-key decryption
305	- FilePointer async resolution: 500ms base * 2^attempt exponential backoff (1s, 2s, 4s) with 3 retries
306	- Removed custom dstack-sdk.d.ts since @phala/dstack-sdk@0.5.7 ships own TypeScript types
307	- Defensive CVM key derivation handles both key (v0.5+) and asUint8Array (legacy) SDK return types
308	- TEE worker Prometheus metrics use `cipherbox_tee_*` prefix for Grafana dashboard coexistence with API metrics
309	- TEE worker structured JSON logger has zero external dependencies (JSON.stringify to stdout/stderr)
310	- [Phase 48-05] Share itemName encrypted at rest via additive nullable item_name_encrypted bytea on BOTH shares and share_invites (decision A3 includes invite flow); migration is additive-only with NO data UPDATE (server zero-knowledge cannot re-encrypt legacy plaintext); itemNameEncrypted optional hex DTO on create-share/create-invite/claim-invite; claim re-wraps ephemeral→recipient ciphertext onto the Share; web encrypt/decrypt/lazy-backfill deferred to 48-06
311	- [Phase 48-06] Web ECIES-wraps itemName on share/invite create (recipient pubkey for direct, ephemeral pubkey for invite) and sends ciphertext-only (itemName: '' + itemNameEncrypted) -- no plaintext display name at rest for new rows; recipient decrypts itemNameEncrypted into the store's plaintext projection on received-share load so display sites are unchanged; owner sent-list uses plaintext fallback (zero-knowledge: name wrapped for recipient, owner can't decrypt -- T-48-18 accept). API GAP: no update endpoint accepts itemNameEncrypted, so the legacy lazy-backfill (A2) is detect+re-wrap only; persist blocked pending a follow-up API plan (PATCH itemNameEncrypted)

338	### Open Concerns
339	
340	- 6 LOW-priority tech debt items remain from M2 audit: Settings URL param parsing, OCC coverage, addManyFiles atomicity, conflict telemetry, lazy rotation, desktop E2E (see `.planning/milestones/m2/m2-v1.0-production-MILESTONE-AUDIT.md`)
341	- Recovery tool subfolder recovery limited by IPNS DHT propagation (root-level fully operational; per-file IPNS records may not be resolvable if not propagated -- architectural limitation, not a bug)

343	  - Q1 (Phase 68): Co-writer offline during write-key rotation -- accept explicit re-fetch requirement or add grace/notification?
344	  - Q2 (Phase 63): Rotation host for pure-web users -- is a long chunked multi-session web rotation acceptable for large revokes, or is desktop the only host?
345	  - Q3 (Phases 65, 68, 69): Write-recipient-vs-owner sub-share authority -- when C (write recipient) deletes a node the owner independently sub-shared to D, who controls revocation of D?

375	## Decisions
376	
377	- [Phase 59-04]: F.1 next_file_publish_sequence(is_first_publish=true) returns 1; unified with TS SDK first-publish convention
378	- [Phase 59-04]: F.2 replay.rs child-folder first-publish embeds seq=1; record_publish seeds at 1 for coordinator consistency
379	- [Phase 59-04]: F.3 verify.rs skew allowance (resp_seq==1 && embedded_seq==0) removed; strict embedded_seq == resp_seq (T-59-10)
380	- [Phase 59-04]: F.4 ipns_verify_vectors case-8 expected_result changed valid->invalid; classify_vector uses strict equality
381	- [Phase 59-04]: TEE re-sign path confirmed NOT hitting upsertFolderIpns embedded-seq gate (T-59-11 accepted); no API/TS change needed
382	- [Phase 59-03]: D.1 journal_entry if/else body collapsed to single Err; param kept with D-01a TODO
383	- [Phase 59-03]: D.3 current_seq_for_cas replaced by direct if current_seq.is_none() guard (same error text)
384	- [Phase 59-03]: E.1 VerifiedResolve::signature_verified field removed; was never read, only written
385	- [Phase 59-03]: E.4 bytesToHex helper removed alongside the unused public_key/private_key vector fields
386	- [Phase 59-02]: VerifyError::Legacy carries { cid: String, sequence_number: String } from bind_verified -- no second resolve_ipns in any Legacy arm (T-59-04 TOCTOU eliminated)
387	- [Phase 59-02]: Display for VerifyError::Legacy includes cid and seq: 'legacy record: all signature fields absent (cid={cid}, seq={sequence_number})'
388	- [Phase 59-02]: events.rs synthetic VerifiedResolve keeps signature_verified: false until Finding E.1 (plan 03) removes the field
389	- [Phase 45-04]: IpnsResolveOutcome lives in error.rs with #[derive(Debug)] only -- not thiserror, it is an outcome not an error
390	- [Phase 45-04]: resolve_ipns_for_replay preserves both contains(not found) and contains(404) predicates to avoid classification regression
391	- [Phase 51-01]: CAS ConflictException (409) check placed before S1 BadRequestException (400) sequence check so concurrent-modification signals remain authoritative; S1 reuses anti-rollback incomingParsed to avoid double parse

1551	COMPLETED_DIR=".planning/todos/completed"
1552	mkdir -p "$COMPLETED_DIR"

1558	  RP=$(awk '/^---/{c++;next} c==1 && /^resolves_phase:/{print $2;exit} c==2{exit}' "$TODO_FILE" 2>/dev/null || true)
1559	  if [ "$RP" = "$PHASE_NUM" ] || [ "$RP" = "\"$PHASE_NUM\"" ]; then
1560	    mv "$TODO_FILE" "$COMPLETED_DIR/"
1561	    CLOSED+=("$(basename "$TODO_FILE")")
1562	  fi

1566	  gsd_run query commit "docs(phase-${PHASE_NUMBER}): auto-close ${#CLOSED[@]} todo(s) resolved by this phase" --files .planning/todos/completed/ .planning/STATE.md|| true

1568	  for f in "${CLOSED[@]}"; do echo "  ✓ $f"; done
1569	fi

1663	/gsd-discuss-phase {next} ${GSD_WS} — start here: discuss next phase before planning  ← recommended
1664	/gsd-plan-phase {next} ${GSD_WS} — plan next phase (skip discuss)
1665	/gsd-execute-phase {next} ${GSD_WS} — execute next phase (skip discuss and plan)
1666	```
1667	
1668	If CONTEXT.md **exists** for the next phase, present:
1669	
1670	```
1671	## ✓ Phase {X}: {Name} Complete
1672	
1673	/gsd-progress ${GSD_WS} — see updated roadmap
1674	/gsd-plan-phase {next} ${GSD_WS} — start here: plan next phase (CONTEXT.md already present)  ← recommended
1675	/gsd-discuss-phase {next} ${GSD_WS} — re-discuss next phase
1676	/gsd-execute-phase {next} ${GSD_WS} — execute next phase (skip planning)
1677	```
1678	
1679	Only suggest the commands listed above. Do not invent or hallucinate command names.
1680	</step>
1681	
1682	</process>
1683	
1684	<context_efficiency>
1685	Orchestrator: ~10-15% context for 200k windows, can use more for 1M+ windows.
1686	Subagents: fresh context each (200k-1M depending on model). No polling (Agent blocks). No context bleed.
1687	
1688	For 1M+ context models, consider:
1689	- Passing richer context (code snippets, dependency outputs) directly to executors instead of just file paths
1690	- Running small phases (≤3 plans, no dependencies) inline without subagent spawning overhead
1691	- Relaxing /clear recommendations — context rot onset is much further out with 5x window
1692	</context_efficiency>
1693	
1694	<failure_handling>
1695	- **Quota / rate-limit (any runtime — #3095):** Agent return body contains a sentinel like `usage limit`, `rate limit`, `429`, `too many requests`, `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED`, `usage_limit_reached`. Route via `gsd-tools.cjs query agent.classify-failure` → `class: "quota-exceeded"`. Do not offer retry-now; the right action is wait-for-reset and resume.
1696	- **classifyHandoffIfNeeded false failure:** Agent reports "failed" but error is `classifyHandoffIfNeeded is not defined` → Claude Code bug, not GSD. Spot-check (SUMMARY exists, commits present) → if pass, treat as success
1697	- **Agent fails mid-plan:** Missing SUMMARY.md → report, ask user how to proceed
1698	- **Dependency chain breaks:** Wave 1 fails → Wave 2 dependents likely fail → user chooses attempt or skip
1699	- **All agents in wave fail:** Systemic issue → stop, report for investigation
1700	- **Checkpoint unresolvable:** "Skip this plan?" or "Abort phase execution?" → record partial progress in STATE.md
1701	</failure_handling>
1702	
1703	<resumption>
1704	Re-run `/gsd-execute-phase {phase}` → discover_plans finds completed SUMMARYs → skips them → resumes from first incomplete plan → continues wave execution.
1705	
1706	STATE.md tracks: last completed plan, current wave, pending checkpoints.
1707	</resumption>
1708	
```

```
1	#!/bin/bash
2	
3	# Check if API source files are staged but generated client files are not
4	# This prevents forgetting to run `pnpm api:generate` after API changes
5	
6	# Files that indicate API changes requiring client regeneration
7	API_PATTERNS=(
8	  "apps/api/src/.*\.dto\.ts"
9	  "apps/api/src/.*\.controller\.ts"
10	  "apps/api/src/.*\.entity\.ts"
11	)
12	
13	# Generated files that should be updated when API changes
14	GENERATED_FILES=(
15	  "packages/api-client/openapi.json"
16	)
17	
18	# Get staged files
19	STAGED_FILES=$(git diff --cached --name-only)
20	
21	# Check if any API source files are staged
22	API_CHANGED=false
23	for pattern in "${API_PATTERNS[@]}"; do
24	  if echo "$STAGED_FILES" | grep -qE "$pattern"; then
25	    API_CHANGED=true
26	    break
27	  fi
28	done
29	
30	if [ "$API_CHANGED" = false ]; then
31	  exit 0
32	fi
33	
34	# API files changed - check if generated files are also staged
35	# Only flag files that have actual uncommitted changes (not already up to date)
36	MISSING_GENERATED=()
37	for file in "${GENERATED_FILES[@]}"; do
38	  if ! echo "$STAGED_FILES" | grep -q "^${file}$"; then
39	    # Only require staging if the file actually has unstaged changes
40	    if git diff --quiet "$file" 2>/dev/null; then
41	      continue  # File is up to date, no staging needed
42	    fi
43	    MISSING_GENERATED+=("$file")
44	  fi
45	done
46	
47	if [ ${#MISSING_GENERATED[@]} -gt 0 ]; then
48	  echo ""
49	  echo "⚠️  API source files changed but generated client files are not staged!"
50	  echo ""
51	  echo "You modified API DTOs, controllers, or entities but didn't regenerate the client."
52	  echo ""
53	  echo "Run this command and stage the changes:"
54	  echo ""
55	  echo "    pnpm api:generate"
56	  echo ""
57	  echo "Missing generated files:"
58	  for file in "${MISSING_GENERATED[@]}"; do
59	    echo "    - $file"
60	  done
61	  echo ""
62	  echo "To skip this check (not recommended), use: git commit --no-verify"
63	  echo ""
64	  exit 1
65	fi
66	
67	exit 0
68	
```

**Two orchestration calls I'm making for this run:**
1. **Worktrees OFF → sequential on the main tree.** This pnpm monorepo has documented worktree fragility (isolated checkouts lack `node_modules`/built dist), so executors run one at a time on the installed main tree. Reliability over parallelism.
2. **Scoped post-wave checks, not root `npm test`.** By design 67-01 slims the API entity while its callers in `republish.service.ts` aren't restored until 67-07 (Wave 2) — so `apps/api` is *intentionally* red between waves 1 and 2. I'll check the packages that must stay green (tee-worker, sdk-core) after Wave 1 and confirm the API typecheck is restored after Wave 2.

31	<objective>
32	Add TEE-internal epoch self-derivation and the refuse-to-renew-stale guard (D-03 / TEE-06 / §6.7-1, §6.7-3). The TEE derives `currentEpoch` from its own clock — never the relay's `currentEpoch`/`previousEpoch` scalars (which are removed from the request contract in 67-06). `decryptWithFallback` is reshaped to take a single `keyEpoch` hint (sourced from `ipns_records.key_epoch`) and refuses keys older than `currentEpoch − 1`, emitting a structured `ReEnrollRequiredError` (the signal whose client consumer is deferred to Phase 68/69).
33	
34	Purpose: Make epoch authority physically internal to the enclave so a compromised relay cannot force re-encryption to a wrong epoch (Elevation), and bound key age so stale keys surface a recovery signal instead of silently renewing.
35	Output: `getInternalCurrentEpoch()` export, reshaped `decryptWithFallback`, `ReEnrollRequiredError`, retargeted `reEncryptForEpoch` call convention, and red→green unit tests.
36	
37	RESEARCH Open Question 1 resolution (baked in): use Option B — keep `keyEpoch` as the ECIES decrypt hint from `ipns_records.key_epoch`; `getInternalCurrentEpoch()` reads `EPOCH_ZERO_TIMESTAMP_MS` (env-configured, fallback returns epoch 1 when unset) and is used ONLY for the floor guard and the re-encryption target — never as the ECIES trial epoch. This avoids the undefined clock-anchor problem while satisfying D-03's internal-derivation intent.

```
1	/**
2	 * Key Manager Service
3	 *
4	 * ECIES decryption of IPNS private keys with epoch fallback and re-encryption.
5	 * - Derives the current epoch internally from the TEE clock (§6.7-1, D-03)
6	 * - Refuses to decrypt keys older than currentEpoch − 1 (hard stale floor)
7	 * - Emits ReEnrollRequiredError for stale keys (structured signal, no key material)
8	 * - Tries keyEpoch hint first, then internalCurrentEpoch (mid-rotation fallback)
9	 * - Re-encrypts old-epoch keys with the current epoch key
10	 *
11	 * SECURITY: Caller is responsible for zeroing returned IPNS private keys after use.
12	 */
13	
14	import { unwrapKey, wrapKey } from '@cipherbox/crypto';
15	import { getKeypair, getInternalCurrentEpoch, getPublicKey } from './tee-keys.js';
16	
17	/**
18	 * Structured error emitted when a relay-supplied IPNS key is too old to renew.
19	 *
20	 * Thrown when keyEpoch < internalCurrentEpoch − 1. The client must re-enroll
21	 * (re-encrypt its IPNS private key with the current TEE epoch public key)
22	 * before republishing can continue.
23	 *
24	 * SECURITY: message names epoch integers ONLY — no key bytes or key material.
25	 */
26	export class ReEnrollRequiredError extends Error {
27	  readonly requiresReEnroll = true;
28	
29	  constructor(
30	    readonly keyEpoch: number,
31	    readonly currentEpoch: number
32	  ) {
33	    super(
34	      `IPNS key epoch ${keyEpoch} is older than grace floor ${currentEpoch - 1} (current epoch: ${currentEpoch}). Re-enrollment required.`
35	    );
36	    this.name = 'ReEnrollRequiredError';
37	  }
38	}
39	
40	/**
41	 * Decrypt an ECIES-encrypted IPNS private key using the specified epoch's private key.
42	 *
43	 * @param encryptedIpnsKey - ECIES ciphertext containing the IPNS Ed25519 private key
44	 * @param epoch - The key epoch to use for decryption
45	 * @returns 32-byte Ed25519 IPNS private key
46	 * @throws Error if decryption fails (wrong epoch or corrupted data)
47	 */
48	export async function decryptIpnsKey(
49	  encryptedIpnsKey: Uint8Array,
50	  epoch: number
51	): Promise<Uint8Array> {
52	  const keypair = await getKeypair(epoch);
53	  try {
54	    return await unwrapKey(encryptedIpnsKey, keypair.privateKey);
55	  } finally {
56	    keypair.privateKey.fill(0);
57	  }
58	}
59	
60	/**
61	 * Decrypt an IPNS key with TEE-internal epoch authority.
62	 *
63	 * Derives the current epoch from the TEE's own clock (never from a relay scalar).
64	 * Refuses keys older than currentEpoch − 1 by throwing ReEnrollRequiredError
65	 * BEFORE any decryption attempt — the hard stale floor (D-03, §6.7-3).
66	 *
67	 * Trial order:
68	 *  1. keyEpoch (the epoch the key is encrypted for, from ipns_records.key_epoch)
69	 *  2. internalCurrentEpoch (mid-rotation fallback: key may have been re-encrypted)
70	 *
71	 * @param encryptedIpnsKey - ECIES ciphertext
72	 * @param keyEpoch - Epoch hint from ipns_records.key_epoch (never relay's currentEpoch)
73	 * @returns Object with decrypted key and which epoch succeeded
74	 * @throws ReEnrollRequiredError if keyEpoch < internalCurrentEpoch − 1 (stale)
75	 * @throws Error if all decryption trials fail (corrupted or unknown key)
76	 */
77	export async function decryptWithFallback(
78	  encryptedIpnsKey: Uint8Array,
79	  keyEpoch: number
80	): Promise<{ ipnsPrivateKey: Uint8Array; usedEpoch: number }> {
81	  const internalCurrentEpoch = getInternalCurrentEpoch();
82	
83	  // Hard stale floor (D-03, §6.7-3): refuse keys older than currentEpoch − 1
84	  // BEFORE any unwrap attempt — a compromised relay cannot force re-encryption
85	  // to a wrong epoch by sending stale keys.
86	  if (keyEpoch < internalCurrentEpoch - 1) {
87	    throw new ReEnrollRequiredError(keyEpoch, internalCurrentEpoch);
88	  }
89	
90	  // Trial 1: keyEpoch (the epoch the key is recorded as encrypted for)
91	  try {
92	    const ipnsPrivateKey = await decryptIpnsKey(encryptedIpnsKey, keyEpoch);
93	    return { ipnsPrivateKey, usedEpoch: keyEpoch };
94	  } catch {
95	    // keyEpoch trial failed — try the internal-current epoch next
96	  }
97	
98	  // Trial 2: internalCurrentEpoch (mid-rotation: key may have been re-encrypted
99	  // to current epoch while keyEpoch still reflects the old DB value)
100	  if (keyEpoch !== internalCurrentEpoch) {
101	    try {
102	      const ipnsPrivateKey = await decryptIpnsKey(encryptedIpnsKey, internalCurrentEpoch);
103	      return { ipnsPrivateKey, usedEpoch: internalCurrentEpoch };
104	    } catch {
105	      // internal-current epoch also failed
106	    }
107	  }
108	
109	  throw new Error('ECIES decryption failed: key may be corrupted or from an unknown epoch');
110	}
111	
112	/**
113	 * Re-encrypt an IPNS private key for a target epoch.
114	 *
115	 * Used during epoch migration: when a key was decrypted with the previous epoch,
116	 * it gets re-encrypted with the target epoch's public key so future republishes
117	 * use the current epoch directly. Callers (e.g. republish route after 67-06)
118	 * should pass getInternalCurrentEpoch() as the targetEpoch.
119	 *
120	 * @param ipnsPrivateKey - Plaintext 32-byte Ed25519 IPNS private key
121	 * @param targetEpoch - The epoch to encrypt for
122	 * @returns ECIES ciphertext encrypted with target epoch's public key
123	 */
124	export async function reEncryptForEpoch(
125	  ipnsPrivateKey: Uint8Array,
126	  targetEpoch: number
127	): Promise<Uint8Array> {
128	  const targetPublicKey = await getPublicKey(targetEpoch);
129	  return await wrapKey(ipnsPrivateKey, targetPublicKey);
130	}
131	
```

1	/**
2	 * IPNS Record Signing Service
3	 *
4	 * Delegates to @cipherbox/core for IPNS record creation and marshaling.
5	 * TEE-specific: Uses 48-hour record lifetime (vs 24h default) to provide
6	 * comfortable margin with 6-hour republish interval.
7	 */
8	
9	import { createIpnsRecord, marshalIpnsRecord } from '@cipherbox/core';
10	import { parseIpnsRecord } from '@cipherbox/crypto';
11	
12	/** 48-hour IPNS record lifetime for TEE-republished records */
13	const TEE_RECORD_LIFETIME_MS = 48 * 60 * 60 * 1000;
14	
15	/**
16	 * Sign an IPNS record with an Ed25519 private key.
17	 *
18	 * Creates a V1+V2 compatible IPNS record pointing to the given CID,
19	 * with a 48-hour lifetime and the specified sequence number.
20	 *
21	 * @param ed25519PrivateKey - 32-byte Ed25519 private key (seed)
22	 * @param cid - IPFS CID string to point to (without /ipfs/ prefix)
23	 * @param sequenceNumber - Monotonically increasing sequence number
24	 * @returns Marshaled (protobuf-encoded) signed IPNS record bytes
25	 */
26	/**
27	 * Renew an existing IPNS record by re-signing it with only a later EOL.
28	 *
29	 * Parses the marshaled existing record and re-signs the SAME value (CID) and
30	 * SAME sequenceNumber with a fresh validity window. This is the pure lease-renew
31	 * primitive: it structurally cannot repoint the CID or increment the sequence
32	 * because value and sequence come exclusively from the parsed existing record.
33	 *
34	 * Security invariants (TEE-01 / TEE-02):
35	 * - value comes from parseIpnsRecord(marshaledExistingRecord).value only
36	 * - sequence comes from parseIpnsRecord(marshaledExistingRecord).sequence only
37	 * - no cid argument, no sequence argument — the relay cannot inject either
38	 *
39	 * @param ed25519PrivateKey - 32-byte Ed25519 private key (seed)
40	 * @param marshaledExistingRecord - Protobuf-encoded IPNS record to renew
41	 * @param lifetimeMs - New validity window in milliseconds (default: 48 hours)
42	 * @returns Marshaled (protobuf-encoded) renewed IPNS record bytes
43	 */
44	export async function renewIpnsRecord(
45	  ed25519PrivateKey: Uint8Array,
46	  marshaledExistingRecord: Uint8Array,
47	  lifetimeMs: number = TEE_RECORD_LIFETIME_MS
48	): Promise<Uint8Array> {
49	  const parsed = await parseIpnsRecord(marshaledExistingRecord);
50	  const record = await createIpnsRecord(
51	    ed25519PrivateKey,
52	    parsed.value,
53	    parsed.sequence,
54	    lifetimeMs
55	  );
56	  return marshalIpnsRecord(record);
57	}
58	
59	export async function signIpnsRecord(
60	  ed25519PrivateKey: Uint8Array,
61	  cid: string,
62	  sequenceNumber: bigint
63	): Promise<Uint8Array> {
64	  const record = await createIpnsRecord(
65	    ed25519PrivateKey,
66	    '/ipfs/' + cid,
67	    sequenceNumber,
68	    TEE_RECORD_LIFETIME_MS
69	  );
70	  return marshalIpnsRecord(record);
71	}
72	
```

32	<objective>
33	Rewrite the TEE republish route into the verify-in-enclave lease renewer (D-01 / TEE-01 / TEE-02 / TEE-06 / §6.4 / §6.7-2 / §7.3 tests 12, 18, 19). The relay sends the marshaled existing `signedRecord` (from `ipns_records.signed_record`) plus the encrypted IPNS key + `keyEpoch`. The TEE: (1) parses the record, (2) verifies its Ed25519 signature against `publicKeyFromIpnsName(ipnsName)`, (3) decrypts the key via the internal-epoch fallback (67-02), (4) asserts the name↔key binding, (5) re-signs the SAME CID + SAME sequence with only a later EOL (67-03's `renewIpnsRecord`), (6) zeroes the key, (7) re-encrypts to the TEE-internal current epoch when upgrading. The relay-supplied `currentEpoch`/`previousEpoch` scalars and the relay-sent CID/sequence are removed from the request contract.
34	
35	Purpose: The contract "cannot originate or repoint a CID" only holds if the TEE validates the incoming signature itself — re-signing a relay-read row would relocate trust to the relay. This is the load-bearing security change of the phase.
36	Output: rewritten `routes/republish.ts` + extended `republish.test.ts` proving no-increment, binding rejection, verify rejection, and re-enroll surfacing.
37	</objective>

Rewrite the per-entry block to: decode `signedRecord` from base64; `parseIpnsRecord` it; `verifyIpnsRecordSignature(entry.ipnsName, bytes)` and reject (continue to the failure result) if false BEFORE any decryption; `decryptWithFallback(encryptedIpnsKey, entry.keyEpoch)`; derive the pubkey from the decrypted key (ed25519 getPublicKey) and compare byte-for-byte against `publicKeyFromIpnsName(entry.ipnsName)` — on mismatch zero the key and reject (do NOT use parsed.pubKey, which is undefined for Ed25519 identity records — RESEARCH Pitfall 1); call `renewIpnsRecord(decryptedKey, bytes, TEE_RECORD_LIFETIME_MS)` for the renewed bytes; set `newSequenceNumber` to the parsed sequence as a string (the re-signed record carries the parsed sequence unchanged — there is NO relay-sent sequence and NO add-one); zero the key immediately after the last use. 1	/**
2	 * Republish Route
3	 *
4	 * POST /republish - Verify-in-enclave IPNS lease-renewer (TEE-01 / TEE-02 / TEE-06).
5	 *
6	 * Contract (D-01): the relay sends the marshaled existing signedRecord and the encrypted
7	 * IPNS private key. The TEE:
8	 *   1. Parses the signedRecord bytes.
9	 *   2. Verifies the Ed25519 signature against publicKeyFromIpnsName(ipnsName) — BEFORE decryption.
10	 *   3. Decrypts the key via the internal-epoch fallback (D-03 / 67-02).
11	 *   4. Asserts the name↔key binding: deriveEd25519PublicKey(decryptedKey) must equal
12	 *      publicKeyFromIpnsName(ipnsName). NEVER uses parsed.pubKey (undefined for Ed25519
13	 *      identity records — RESEARCH Pitfall 1).
14	 *   5. Re-signs the SAME value (CID) + SAME sequence with only a later EOL via renewIpnsRecord.
15	 *      newSequenceNumber == parsedSequence — no increment (TEE-02 / §7.3 test 12).
16	 *   6. Optionally re-encrypts the key for the current internal epoch (D-03).
17	 *   7. Zeros the decrypted key on EVERY path (success, binding-fail, re-enroll, error).
18	 *
19	 * REMOVED from request body: latestCid, sequenceNumber, currentEpoch, previousEpoch.
20	 * The relay MUST NOT send those fields; the TEE MUST NOT read them.
21	 *
22	 * Each entry is processed independently — one failure does not block others.
23	 *
24	 * SECURITY:
25	 * - IPNS private keys are zeroed immediately after their last use (T-67-06-I)
26	 * - No key material appears in logs or error messages
27	 * - Signature verification precedes decryption (T-67-06-T)
28	 * - Binding check uses name-derived key, never parsed.pubKey (T-67-06-T2)
29	 * - Epoch upgrade uses TEE-internal clock, never relay scalars (T-67-06-E)
30	 */
31	
32	import { Router, type Request, type Response } from 'express';
33	import {
34	  parseIpnsRecord,
35	  verifyIpnsRecordSignature,
36	  publicKeyFromIpnsName,
37	  deriveEd25519PublicKey,
38	} from '@cipherbox/crypto';
39	import {
40	  decryptWithFallback,
41	  reEncryptForEpoch,
42	  ReEnrollRequiredError,
43	} from '../services/key-manager.js';
44	import { renewIpnsRecord } from '../services/ipns-signer.js';
45	import { getInternalCurrentEpoch } from '../services/tee-keys.js';
46	import { republishEntries } from '../middleware/metrics.js';
47	import { logger } from '../services/logger.js';
48	
49	const router = Router();
50	
51	/**
52	 * Shape of each entry in the republish request (D-01 / D-02 / D-03 contract).
53	 *
54	 * Removed from v1: latestCid, sequenceNumber, currentEpoch, previousEpoch.
55	 * All signing inputs come from the marshaled signedRecord; epoch is self-derived.
56	 */
57	interface RepublishEntry {
58	  encryptedIpnsKey: string; // base64-encoded ECIES ciphertext of the IPNS Ed25519 private key
59	  keyEpoch: number; // epoch the key is recorded as encrypted for (from ipns_records.key_epoch)
60	  ipnsName: string; // IPNS name (CIDv1 base36)
61	  signedRecord: string; // base64-encoded marshaled existing IPNS record
62	}
63	
64	/** Shape of each result in the republish response */
65	interface RepublishResult {
66	  ipnsName: string;
67	  success: boolean;
68	  signedRecord?: string; // base64-encoded marshaled renewed IPNS record (same CID + seq, later EOL)
69	  newSequenceNumber?: string; // equals the parsed input sequence (no increment)
70	  upgradedEncryptedKey?: string; // base64, present if key was re-encrypted for current epoch
71	  upgradedKeyEpoch?: number;
72	  requiresReEnroll?: true; // present when ReEnrollRequiredError thrown (T-67-06-I)
73	  error?: string;
74	}
75	
76	router.post('/republish', async (req: Request, res: Response) => {
77	  const { entries } = req.body as { entries: RepublishEntry[] };
78	
79	  if (!entries || !Array.isArray(entries)) {
80	    res.status(400).json({ error: 'Missing or invalid entries array' });
81	    return;
82	  }
83	
84	  const MAX_BATCH_SIZE = 100;
85	  if (entries.length > MAX_BATCH_SIZE) {
86	    res
87	      .status(400)
88	      .json({ error: `Batch too large: ${entries.length} entries (max ${MAX_BATCH_SIZE})` });
89	    return;
90	  }
91	
92	  const results: RepublishResult[] = [];
93	
94	  for (const entry of entries) {
95	    let ipnsPrivateKey: Uint8Array | null = null;
96	
97	    try {
98	      // ── Step 1: Decode the marshaled IPNS record ────────────────────────────
99	      const signedRecordBytes = Buffer.from(entry.signedRecord, 'base64');
100	
101	      // ── Step 2: Parse the record to extract value (CID) + sequence ──────────
102	      // Must succeed BEFORE any signature check or decryption.
103	      const parsed = await parseIpnsRecord(signedRecordBytes);
104	
105	      // ── Step 3: Verify Ed25519 signature against publicKeyFromIpnsName ───────
106	      // T-67-06-T: relay cannot forge a signed record; verification PRECEDES decryption.
107	      // verifyIpnsRecordSignature returns false on any error — never throws.
108	      const isValid = await verifyIpnsRecordSignature(entry.ipnsName, signedRecordBytes);
109	      if (!isValid) {
110	        // Reject before any decryption — decrypt spy MUST be uncalled (§7.3 test 18)
111	        results.push({
112	          ipnsName: entry.ipnsName,
113	          success: false,
114	          error: 'IPNS signature verification failed',
115	        });
116	        republishEntries.inc({ result: 'failure' });
117	        continue;
118	      }
119	
120	      // ── Step 4: Decrypt IPNS key using internal-epoch authority (D-03 / 67-02) ─
121	      const encryptedIpnsKey = new Uint8Array(Buffer.from(entry.encryptedIpnsKey, 'base64'));
122	      const { ipnsPrivateKey: decryptedKey, usedEpoch } = await decryptWithFallback(
123	        encryptedIpnsKey,
124	        entry.keyEpoch
125	      );
126	      ipnsPrivateKey = decryptedKey;
127	
128	      // ── Step 5: Name↔key binding assertion (§6.7-2 / T-67-06-S / T-67-06-T2) ─
129	      // Derive the Ed25519 public key from the DECRYPTED key — NEVER from parsed.pubKey
130	      // (which is undefined for Ed25519 identity records — RESEARCH Pitfall 1).
131	      const derivedPubkey = deriveEd25519PublicKey(ipnsPrivateKey);
132	      const namePubkey = publicKeyFromIpnsName(entry.ipnsName);
133	      if (!derivedPubkey.every((b, i) => b === namePubkey[i])) {
134	        // Zero key before rejecting — T-67-06-I
135	        ipnsPrivateKey.fill(0);
136	        ipnsPrivateKey = null;
137	        results.push({
138	          ipnsName: entry.ipnsName,
139	          success: false,
140	          error: 'Name-key binding violation',
141	        });
142	        republishEntries.inc({ result: 'failure' });
143	        continue;
144	      }
145	
146	      // ── Step 6: Re-sign — SAME value (CID) + SAME sequence + later EOL ──────
147	      // renewIpnsRecord parses value+sequence from the existing record internally;
148	      // the relay cannot inject a different CID or sequence (TEE-01 / TEE-02).
149	      const renewedBytes = await renewIpnsRecord(ipnsPrivateKey, signedRecordBytes);
150	
151	      // ── Step 7: Epoch upgrade (T-67-06-E / D-03) ────────────────────────────
152	      // Target is the TEE-internal current epoch — NEVER a relay-supplied scalar.
153	      // Re-encrypt BEFORE zeroing so the key is still available for wrapKey.
154	      let upgradedEncryptedKey: string | undefined;
155	      let upgradedKeyEpoch: number | undefined;
156	      const internalCurrentEpoch = getInternalCurrentEpoch();
157	      if (usedEpoch !== internalCurrentEpoch) {
158	        const reEncrypted = await reEncryptForEpoch(ipnsPrivateKey, internalCurrentEpoch);
159	        upgradedEncryptedKey = Buffer.from(reEncrypted).toString('base64');
160	        upgradedKeyEpoch = internalCurrentEpoch;
161	      }
162	
163	      // ── Step 8: Zero the key immediately after last use (T-67-06-I) ─────────
164	      ipnsPrivateKey.fill(0);
165	      ipnsPrivateKey = null;
166	
167	      // ── Step 9: Build success result ─────────────────────────────────────────
168	      // newSequenceNumber == parsed.sequence (no +1) — §7.3 test 12
169	      const result: RepublishResult = {
170	        ipnsName: entry.ipnsName,
171	        success: true,
172	        signedRecord: Buffer.from(renewedBytes).toString('base64'),
173	        newSequenceNumber: parsed.sequence.toString(),
174	      };
175	      if (upgradedEncryptedKey) {
176	        result.upgradedEncryptedKey = upgradedEncryptedKey;
177	        result.upgradedKeyEpoch = upgradedKeyEpoch;
178	      }
179	
180	      results.push(result);
181	      republishEntries.inc({ result: 'success' });
182	    } catch (error) {
183	      // Ensure key is zeroed even on unexpected error (T-67-06-I)
184	      if (ipnsPrivateKey) {
185	        ipnsPrivateKey.fill(0);
186	        ipnsPrivateKey = null;
187	      }
188	
189	      // ReEnrollRequiredError → structured flag, no key material (§7.3 test 19)
190	      const isReEnroll = error instanceof ReEnrollRequiredError;
191	      const result: RepublishResult = {
192	        ipnsName: entry.ipnsName,
193	        success: false,
194	        error: isReEnroll
195	          ? 'RE_ENROLL_REQUIRED'
196	          : error instanceof Error
197	            ? error.message
198	            : 'Unknown error',
199	      };
200	      if (isReEnroll) {
201	        result.requiresReEnroll = true;
202	      }
203	      results.push(result);
204	      republishEntries.inc({ result: 'failure' });
205	    }
206	  }
207	
208	  const successes = results.filter((r) => r.success).length;
209	
210	  logger.info('Republish batch complete', {
211	    total: entries.length,
212	    succeeded: successes,
213	    failed: entries.length - successes,
214	  });
215	
216	  res.json({ results });
217	});
218	
219	export default router;
220	
```

| Binding via `deriveEd25519PublicKey(decryptedKey)` vs `publicKeyFromIpnsName` — **not** `parsed.pubKey` | :131-133 | ✓ |

| `ReEnrollRequiredError` → `requiresReEnroll:true`, error `'RE_ENROLL_REQUIRED'`, no key bytes | :190-202 | ✓ |

## 67-08 Live Gate — Runbook

**0. Rebuild the dists the round-trip depends on** (sdk-core changed in 67-04; the e2e imports its built dist)
```bash
pnpm --filter @cipherbox/sdk-core build
```

**1. Build + start the *current* tee-worker** (the one running is 2-3 weeks stale, pre-67-06)
```bash
docker compose -f docker/docker-compose.yml up -d --build tee-worker
docker compose -f docker/docker-compose.yml ps tee-worker   # wait for "healthy"
```

**2. `[BLOCKING]` Run the migration** (irreversible column drop on local Postgres)
```bash
cd apps/api && pnpm migration:run
# expect: ScheduleCollapse1751000000000 applied
```
Confirm the 4 columns are gone:
```bash
docker compose -f docker/docker-compose.yml exec postgres \
  psql -U postgres -d cipherbox -c \
  "select column_name from information_schema.columns where table_name='ipns_republish_schedule' and column_name in ('encrypted_ipns_key','key_epoch','latest_cid','sequence_number');"
# expect: 0 rows
```

**3. Start the API from current source, wired to the new tee-worker** (restart if an older `api dev` is running — it must carry the 67-07 relay code)
```bash
# ensure the relay targets :3002 (the real tee-worker), NOT the mock on :3001
TEE_WORKER_URL=http://localhost:3002 pnpm --filter @cipherbox/api dev
```
> ⚠️ Critical wiring: if your `apps/api/.env` has `TEE_WORKER_URL` unset or `:3001`, the relay hits `mock-ipns-routing` instead of the TEE. It must be `http://localhost:3002`.

**4. Run the round-trip suite** (new terminal; redis 6380 is up)
```bash
pnpm --filter @cipherbox/sdk-e2e test -- tee-republish
```
**Expected:** both tests pass — Test A: renewed record has **equal sequence**, **equal CID**, **later EOL** (bytes differ); Test B: tombstoned name **not re-signed** (`signed_record` unchanged, schedule row gone). Sanity: tee-worker logs show a batch processed, **no key material logged, no sequence increment**.

8	 *     differs; assert renewed.sequence === original.sequence (no increment), renewed.value ===
9	 *     original.value (same CID), and the renewed bytes differ (later EOL).
10	 *   Test B (tombstone never re-signed): Enroll a second record with TEE keys; tombstone the
11	 *     name via POST /ipns/tombstone; assert ipns_republish_schedule row is absent (unenrolled
12	 *     by tombstone handler); enqueue ONE job; assert the signed_record is unchanged.
13	 *
14	 * These two cases need the live stack because they exercise the real relay→TEE→DB round-trip.
15	 * The determinism is fully test-owned: no timer/scheduler wait; exactly ONE BullMQ job per test.

23	import { afterAll, beforeAll, describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';

35	const DB_CONFIG: pg.PoolConfig = {

38	  user: process.env.DB_USERNAME ?? 'postgres',
39	  password: process.env.DB_PASSWORD ?? 'postgres',

46	};

57	  pool = new pg.Pool(DB_CONFIG);
58	});
59	
60	afterAll(async () => {
61	  if (fixture) await fixture.cleanupAll();
62	  if (pool) await pool.end();
63	});

65	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
66	// Helpers
67	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
68	
69	/**
70	 * Read the TEE public key + current epoch from tee_key_state.
71	 * Returns an object compatible with the TeeKeys type expected by createSubfolder.
72	 */
73	async function readTeeKeys(): Promise<{ currentPublicKey: string; currentEpoch: number }> {
74	  const result = await pool.query<{ public_key: string; current_epoch: number }>(

91	 * Returns null if no row exists.

94	  const result = await pool.query<{ signed_record: Buffer | null }>(

97	  );

104	 */

118	async function enqueueRepublishBatch(): Promise<void> {

136	  /**
137	   * Initialize TEE key state from the TEE worker on module startup.
138	   * If tee_key_state is empty, queries TEE for epoch 1 public key and initializes.
139	   * If tee_key_state has data, validates it still matches the TEE worker.
140	   * Gracefully handles TEE worker being unavailable (logs warning, does not throw).
141	   */
142	  async initializeFromTee(): Promise<void> {
143	    try {
144	      // Check TEE worker health first
145	      const health = await this.getHealth();
146	      this.logger.log(`TEE worker healthy, current epoch: ${health.epoch}`);
147	
148	      const currentState = await this.teeKeyStateService.getCurrentState();
149	
150	      if (!currentState) {
151	        // First boot: initialize from TEE worker
152	        const publicKey = await this.getPublicKey(health.epoch);
153	        await this.teeKeyStateService.initializeEpoch(health.epoch, publicKey);
154	        this.logger.log(`TEE key state initialized from worker at epoch ${health.epoch}`);
155	        return;
156	      }
157	
158	      // Validate existing state matches TEE worker
159	      if (currentState.currentEpoch !== health.epoch) {
160	        this.logger.warn(
161	          `TEE epoch mismatch: DB has epoch ${currentState.currentEpoch}, TEE worker reports epoch ${health.epoch}. ` +
162	            'This may indicate a TEE worker update. Manual epoch rotation may be needed.'
163	        );
164	      } else {
165	        this.logger.log(
166	          `TEE key state validated: epoch ${currentState.currentEpoch} matches worker`
167	        );
168	      }
169	    } catch (error) {
170	      // TEE worker unavailable - this is expected during development
171	      // and acceptable for startup (republishing will retry later)
172	      const message = error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error);
173	      this.logger.warn(`TEE worker unavailable during initialization: ${message}`);
174	      this.logger.warn(
175	        'TEE republishing will not work until the TEE worker is available. ' +
176	          'This is expected in development without a TEE simulator.'
177	      );
178	    }
179	  }
180	
181	  /**
182	   * Forward a connection test to the TEE worker.
183	   * The TEE worker decrypts ECIES-encrypted provider config in-enclave,
184	   * probes the endpoint server-side, and returns the result.
185	   */
186	  async connectionTest(encryptedConfig: string, epoch: number): Promise<ConnectionTestResponseDto> {
187	    const response = await this.fetchWithTimeout(`${this.teeWorkerUrl}/connection-test`, {
188	      method: 'POST',
189	      headers: {
190	        ...this.authHeaders(),
191	        'Content-Type': 'application/json',
192	      },
193	      body: JSON.stringify({ encryptedConfig, epoch }),
194	    });
195	
196	    if (!response.ok) {
197	      const errorText = await response.text();
198	      return {
199	        success: false,
200	        latencyMs: 0,
201	        error: `TEE worker error: ${response.status} - ${errorText}`,
202	      };
203	    }
204	
205	    return (await response.json()) as ConnectionTestResponseDto;
206	  }
207	
208	  /**
209	   * Build authorization headers for TEE worker requests.
210	   * IMPORTANT: Never log the secret value.
211	   */
212	  private authHeaders(): Record<string, string> {
213	    const headers: Record<string, string> = {};
214	    if (this.teeWorkerSecret) {
215	      headers['Authorization'] = `Bearer ${this.teeWorkerSecret}`;
216	    }
217	    return headers;
218	  }

1	/**
2	 * TEE Key Derivation Service
3	 *
4	 * Derives deterministic secp256k1 keypairs per epoch.
5	 * - Simulator mode: HKDF-SHA256 from a fixed seed (development/testing)
6	 * - CVM mode: DstackClient.getKey() for hardware-backed derivation (production)
7	 */
8	
9	import { hkdf } from '@noble/hashes/hkdf';
10	import { sha256 } from '@noble/hashes/sha256';
11	import * as secp from '@noble/secp256k1';
12	
13	/** Cache public keys per epoch to avoid repeated derivation */
14	const publicKeyCache = new Map<number, Uint8Array>();
15	const MAX_CACHE_SIZE = 100;
16	
17	/** Valid epoch range — epochs are small sequential integers (one per ~4 weeks) */
18	export const MIN_EPOCH = 1;
19	export const MAX_EPOCH = 10_000;
20	
21	/**
22	 * 4-week epoch duration in milliseconds.
23	 * Must match the relay's epoch schedule configuration.
24	 */
25	export const EPOCH_DURATION_MS = 4 * 7 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000;
26	
27	/**
28	 * Derive the current epoch from the TEE's own clock.
29	 *
30	 * Reads EPOCH_ZERO_TIMESTAMP_MS from the environment at call time (not module
31	 * load) so tests and staged rollouts can vary it without restarting the process.
32	 * Returns MIN_EPOCH (1) when the anchor is absent or in the future — a safe
33	 * fallback floor that never triggers the stale-key guard.
34	 *
35	 * This is the §6.7-1 internal-clock derivation — it never reads a relay-supplied
36	 * currentEpoch/previousEpoch scalar.
37	 *
38	 * @returns Current epoch number (≥ MIN_EPOCH)
39	 */
40	export function getInternalCurrentEpoch(): number {
41	  const anchor = parseInt(process.env.EPOCH_ZERO_TIMESTAMP_MS ?? '0', 10);
42	  if (anchor === 0) {
43	    return MIN_EPOCH; // Safe fallback: no anchor configured → epoch 1
44	  }
45	  return Math.max(MIN_EPOCH, Math.floor((Date.now() - anchor) / EPOCH_DURATION_MS) + 1);
46	}
47	
48	/**
49	 * Derive a deterministic secp256k1 keypair for a given epoch.
50	 *
51	 * In simulator mode, uses HKDF-SHA256 with a fixed seed.
52	 * In CVM mode, uses Phala dstack SDK for hardware-backed derivation.
53	 *
54	 * @param epoch - The key epoch number
55	 * @returns Object with publicKey (65 bytes, uncompressed) and privateKey (32 bytes)
56	 */
57	export async function getKeypair(
58	  epoch: number
59	): Promise<{ publicKey: Uint8Array; privateKey: Uint8Array }> {
60	  const mode = process.env.TEE_MODE || 'simulator';
61	
62	  const env = process.env.CIPHERBOX_ENVIRONMENT;
63	  if (
64	    mode === 'simulator' &&
65	    (env === 'production' || (!env && process.env.NODE_ENV === 'production'))
66	  ) {
67	    throw new Error(
68	      'TEE_MODE=simulator is not allowed in production. Set TEE_MODE=cvm for production deployments, or set CIPHERBOX_ENVIRONMENT explicitly.'
69	    );
70	  }
71	
72	  let privateKey: Uint8Array;
73	
74	  if (mode === 'cvm') {
75	    // Production: Phala Cloud CVM with dstack SDK
76	    // Dynamic import -- @phala/dstack-sdk is only available inside CVM
77	    const { DstackClient } = await import('@phala/dstack-sdk');
78	    const client = new DstackClient();
79	    const keyResult = await client.getKey('cipherbox/ipns-republish', `epoch-${epoch}`);
80	
81	    // Defensive handling: SDK v0.5+ returns { key: Uint8Array },
82	    // older versions returned { asUint8Array(): Uint8Array }
83	    const keyAny = keyResult as unknown as Record<string, unknown>;
84	    const rawKey =
85	      'key' in keyResult && keyResult.key instanceof Uint8Array
86	        ? keyResult.key
87	        : typeof keyAny.asUint8Array === 'function'
88	          ? (keyAny.asUint8Array as () => Uint8Array)()
89	          : (() => {
90	              throw new Error('Unexpected DstackClient.getKey() return type');
91	            })();
92	    privateKey = new Uint8Array(rawKey.slice(0, 32));
93	  } else {
94	    // Simulator: deterministic HKDF derivation from fixed seed
95	    const seed = new TextEncoder().encode('cipherbox-tee-simulator-seed');
96	    const salt = new TextEncoder().encode('cipherbox-dev');
97	    const info = new TextEncoder().encode(`epoch-${epoch}`);
98	    privateKey = hkdf(sha256, seed, salt, info, 32);
99	  }
100	
101	  // Derive uncompressed public key (65 bytes, 0x04 prefix)
102	  const publicKey = secp.getPublicKey(privateKey, false);
103	
104	  // Cache public key for this epoch (bounded to prevent memory DoS)
105	  if (publicKeyCache.size >= MAX_CACHE_SIZE) {
106	    const firstKey = publicKeyCache.keys().next().value;
107	    if (firstKey !== undefined) publicKeyCache.delete(firstKey);
108	  }
109	  publicKeyCache.set(epoch, publicKey);
110	
111	  return { publicKey, privateKey };
112	}
113	
114	/**
115	 * Get the cached public key for an epoch, or derive it.
116	 *
117	 * @param epoch - The key epoch number
118	 * @returns 65-byte uncompressed secp256k1 public key
119	 */
120	export async function getPublicKey(epoch: number): Promise<Uint8Array> {
121	  const cached = publicKeyCache.get(epoch);
122	  if (cached) {
123	    return cached;
124	  }
125	
126	  const { publicKey } = await getKeypair(epoch);
127	  return publicKey;
128	}
129

> AGENT

The user asked me to restart the API — that aligns exactly with what's needed (it's stale `node dist/main`). Two facts that de-risk this whole gate:

1. **The simulator TEE key is deterministic** — HKDF from a fixed seed `'cipherbox-tee-simulator-seed'` (`tee-keys.ts:94-98`). So a freshly-built tee-worker produces the **same** secp256k1 key as before → the existing `tee_key_state` will still match, no binding mismatch, no reset needed.
2. **`initializeFromTee()`** re-validates `tee_key_state` against the worker on API startup — so restarting the API after the worker is up self-heals the key state.

13	 */
14	@Entity('tee_key_state')

16	  @PrimaryGeneratedColumn('uuid')

45	   * When the previous epoch's grace period ends.
46	   * After this timestamp, the previous epoch key is deprecated.

124	/**
125	 * Poll predicate until it returns true or the timeout is reached.
126	 * Throws if the timeout expires.
127	 */
128	async function waitFor(
129	  predicate: () => Promise<boolean>,
130	  options: { timeout: number; interval?: number; label?: string } = { timeout: 15_000 }
131	): Promise<void> {
132	  const interval = options.interval ?? 500;
133	  const deadline = Date.now() + options.timeout;
134	  while (Date.now() < deadline) {
135	    if (await predicate()) return;
136	    await new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, interval));
137	  }
138	  throw new Error(
139	    `waitFor timed out after ${options.timeout}ms${options.label ? ` (${options.label})` : ''}`
140	  );
141	}
142	
143	/**
144	 * Check whether an ipns_republish_schedule row exists for the given ipnsName.

146	async function scheduleRowExists(ipnsName: string): Promise<boolean> {
147	  const result = await pool.query<{ count: string }>(
148	    'SELECT COUNT(*)::text AS count FROM ipns_republish_schedule WHERE ipns_name = $1',
149	    [ipnsName]
150	  );
151	  return parseInt(result.rows[0]?.count ?? '0', 10) > 0;
152	}

> AGENT

Found it: the e2e sends `secret = SDK_E2E_SECRET ?? 'e2e-test-secret-do-not-use-in-production'`; the API checks it against `TEST_LOGIN_SECRET`. They don't match because I started the API without pinning that secret. Cleanest fix: restart the API with `TEST_LOGIN_SECRET` set to the e2e's default, so both sides agree. > AGENT

**Found the real bug.** The RepublishProcessor consumes the job fine (redis OK, enrollment OK), but `processRepublishBatch` throws:

```
Republish job failed: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'databaseName')
```

That's a **TypeORM query-builder metadata error** in 67-07's `getDueEntries` JOIN — and it's exactly the class of bug the unit tests can't catch because they *mock* the query builder (this is why the sdk-e2e round-trip is the real gate). Let me read the actual `getDueEntries` implementation.

> AGENT

`getDueEntries` uses `.innerJoin(IpnsRecord, 'r', ...).orderBy('s.next_republish_at').take(2000).getMany()`. The `databaseName` error is TypeORM failing to resolve a **raw DB column name in the take-paginated order/join path** — a metadata pitfall the mocked unit test never exercised. Let me see how the *original* (pre-67-07) query was written and the entity property names, so I fix it to the codebase's working convention rather than guess.

> AGENT

This is the crux bug and the root-cause is clear: **67-07 replaced the original clean `.find()` query with a fragile query-builder `innerJoin` that uses raw DB column names + `take` pagination** — TypeORM can't resolve the metadata (`databaseName`), so `processRepublishBatch` throws before it ever reaches the TEE. The original (pre-67-07) `getDueEntries` used the robust `.find()` FindOptions API. I'll restore that convention while keeping the `ipns_records` tombstone+key pairing. Entity property names confirmed (`tombstonedAt`, `encryptedIpnsPrivateKey`, etc.).

ipnsRecordRepository.find.mockResolvedValue([record]);\n\n      const result = await service.getDueEntries();\n\n      expect(result).toHaveLength(1);\n      expect(result[0]).toMatchObject({ schedule, record });\n    });\n\n    it('should exclude tombstoned names — a tombstoned record yields no pair', async () => {\n      // Schedule is due, but the ipns_records filter (tombstonedAt IS NULL) excludes\n      // the record, so it never enters the record map → the schedule drops out of the\n      // paired result (defense layer 1).\n      const schedule = createMockSchedule();\n      scheduleRepository.find.mockResolvedValue([schedule]);\n      ipnsRecordRepository.find.mockResolvedValue([]);\n\n      const result = await service.getDueEntries();\n\n      expect(result).toEqual([]);\n    });\n\n    it('should filter to active/retrying statuses and next_republish_at <= now', async () => {\n      scheduleRepository.find.mockResolvedValue([]);\n\n      await service.getDueEntries();\n\n      expect(scheduleRepository.find).toHaveBeenCalledWith(\n        expect.objectContaining({\n          where: expect.objectContaining({\n            status: expect.anything(),\n            nextRepublishAt: expect.anything(),\n          }),\n          order: { nextRepublishAt: 'ASC' },\n          take: 2000,\n        })\n      );\n    });\n\n    it('should skip races: schedules returned but record not found → excluded from pairs', async () => {\n      const schedule = createMockSchedule({ ipnsName: 'k51testRaceWindow' });\n      scheduleRepository.find.mockResolvedValue([schedule]);\n      // Record was tombstoned/removed between the two queries (race window)\n      ipnsRecordRepository.find.mockResolvedValue([]);\n\n      const result = await service.getDueEntries();\n\n      expect(result).toHaveLength(0);\n    });"
}

36	# Phase 67 Plan 07: Relay Contract Rewrite — ipns_records as Sole Signing Source Summary
37	
38	Reshaped the relay so the canonical `ipns_records` row is the sole source of the TEE's
39	signing inputs (D-02 / TEE-03) and hardened the EOL-only renewal write with an equality
40	CAS replacing the weak `LessThanOrEqual` write-back (§6.6 / TEE-04 carryover).
41	
42	## Tasks Completed
43	
44	### Task 1 (TDD RED): getDueEntries JOIN + teeEntries rebuild spec
45	
46	Rewrote `republish.service.spec.ts` to reflect the new contract:
47	
48	- Removed `createMockEntry` references to dropped schedule fields (`encryptedIpnsKey`,
49	  `keyEpoch`, `latestCid`, `sequenceNumber`) which 67-01 removed from the entity.
50	- Added `createMockRecord()` helper for `IpnsRecord` pairs.
51	- Set up QB mock chain: `scheduleQBMock` (innerJoin), `recordSelectQBMock`, and
52	  `recordUpdateQBMock` (renewIpnsRecordEol UPDATE).
53	- Added tests: innerJoin condition with tombstone+key filter, paired `{schedule, record}`
54	  return shape, tombstone-exclusion, teeEntries sourced from record (signedRecord,
55	  encryptedIpnsKey, keyEpoch — no latestCid/sequenceNumber/currentEpoch/previousEpoch).
56	
57	Commit: `991afdbbc` — 27 tests failing (RED confirmed).
58

60	
61	`tee.service.ts` — reshaped interfaces:
62	
63	- `RepublishEntry`: removed `latestCid`, `sequenceNumber`, `currentEpoch`, `previousEpoch`;
64	  added `signedRecord: string` (base64 of `ipns_records.signed_record`).
65	- `RepublishResult`: added `requiresReEnroll?: true`.
66	
67	`republish.service.ts` — full rewrite:
68	
69	- `getDueEntries()` → `Promise<DueEntryPair[]>` using two-step QB:
70	  1. Schedule QB with `innerJoin(IpnsRecord, 'r', '... tombstoned_at IS NULL AND encrypted_ipns_private_key IS NOT NULL')` filter.
71	  2. Record QB with same tombstone+key filter on the matched names (race window guard).
72	  Returns `{ schedule, record }` pairs only.
73	
74	- `processRepublishBatch()`:
75	  - Removed `teeKeyStateService.getCurrentState()` from batch path (D-03: TEE self-derives epoch).
76	  - Rebuilds `teeEntries` from `pair.record` (all signing inputs canonical).
77	  - `scheduleRepository.save()` writes ONLY scheduling fields (no crypto columns).
78	  - `requiresReEnroll` → log + `handleEntryFailure` (non-fatal, Phase 68/69).
79	  - Epoch upgrade → `ipnsRecordRepository.update({ipnsName}, {encryptedIpnsPrivateKey, keyEpoch})`.
80	
81	- `renewIpnsRecordEol(ipnsName, loadedSeq, renewedSignedRecord)`:
82	  - Replaces `syncIpnsRecordSequence` and its `LessThanOrEqual` write-back.
83	  - QB UPDATE `signed_record` WHERE `sequence_number = :expected AND tombstoned_at IS NULL`.
84	  - `affected === 0` → log debug + return (forward-publish race or tombstone: harmless discard, no throw).
85	  - `affected > 0` → EOL renewal written (same CID, same seq, later EOL bytes).
86	  - Wrapped in try/catch: non-fatal, publish already succeeded.
87	
88	- `enrollFolder(userId, ipnsName)`: collapsed to 2 args (scheduling-only).
89	
90	Commit: `8daa66a16` — all 39 tests passing (GREEN confirmed).

<system-reminder>This memory is 8 days old. Memories are point-in-time observations, not live state — claims about code behavior or file:line citations may be outdated. Verify against current code before asserting as fact.</system-reminder>
1	---
2	name: project-sdk-e2e-only-cross-package-publish-gate
3	description: SDK E2E is the only gate exercising the real client→API publish round-trip; mock-heavy unit tests miss integration regressions
4	metadata: 
5	  node_type: memory
6	  type: project
7	  originSessionId: ca575d75-db2f-4c2d-8294-09d1af4240cb
8	---
9	
10	The `tests/sdk-e2e` suite (full stack: api + sdk + sdk-core + core + crypto + api-client) is the
11	ONLY gate that exercises the real client→API IPNS publish/resolve round-trip. cargo, jest, and
12	vitest unit suites all mock the boundary. Phase 51 shipped green on all unit suites but broke 48/89
13	SDK E2E because the unit tests mocked `parseIpnsRecord` and used throwaway key buffers.
14	
15	**Why:** the publish path crosses 6 packages and the key-lifecycle + sequence/CID conventions only
16	line up at integration; unit mocks hide both. The SDK E2E run does NOT gate PRs by default the way
17	unit suites do — check it explicitly on any change to IPNS publish/resolve or key lifecycle.
18	
19	**How to apply:** before shipping any change to IPNS publish/resolve, sequence/CAS, or key
20	zeroization, run SDK E2E locally. Prereqs are usually already up (postgres 5432, kubo 5001, redis
21	**6380**, mock-ipns-routing 3001 — note redis is 6380 not 6379). Recipe:
22	
23	1. Rebuild the client chain so dist matches CI: `pnpm --filter @cipherbox/crypto build` then core,
24	   api-client, sdk-core, sdk, api (in that order).
25	2. Start the API on :3000: `cd apps/api && PORT=3000 node dist/main.js` (kill anything on 3000 first).
26	3. Run: `SDK_E2E_API_URL=http://localhost:3000 SDK_E2E_SECRET=<TEST_LOGIN_SECRET from apps/api/.env> pnpm --filter @cipherbox/sdk-e2e test`
27	
28	To capture the real 400 reason (the API does NOT log handled 4xx), temporarily add an axios response
29	interceptor in `packages/api-client/src/instance.ts` that logs `err.response.data`, rebuild the client
30	chain, run one suite, then revert.
31	
32	**Secret-mismatch trap (Phase 58, 2026-06-22):** the concrete value is `TEST_LOGIN_SECRET=[REDACTED]`
33	in `apps/api/.env`, but the suite DEFAULTS `SDK_E2E_SECRET` to `e2e-test-secret-do-not-use-in-production`
34	and **vitest 3 does NOT auto-load `tests/sdk-e2e/.env`** (no dotenv in that package) — so you MUST pass it
35	inline: `SDK_E2E_SECRET=[REDACTED] pnpm --filter @cipherbox/sdk-e2e test`. If they mismatch,
36	`/auth/test-login` returns 401 and EVERY suite skips/false-fails. A 58-02 executor misread this exact 401
37	as "infra-limited" and accepted the gate unrun — do NOT accept a 401-skipped E2E run as infra-limited; it
38	is almost always just the secret. With the secret correct the suite passes 89/89 against the already-running
39	:3000 API (no rebuild/restart needed if the running API already uses that secret).
40	
41	See [project-zeroization-callee-must-not-zero-reused-buffer]] and
42	[[feedback-sdk-integration-tests-need-local-api]].
43	
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/myankelev/.claude/projects/-Users-myankelev-Code-random-cipher-box/memory/project-tee-republish-e2e-stack-recipe.md",
  "content": "---\nname: project-tee-republish-e2e-stack-recipe\ndescription: Concrete local-stack recipe to run the sdk-e2e TEE republish round-trip (phase 67+); the fiddly wiring that took a full debug session\nmetadata:\n  node_type: memory\n  type: project\n---\n\nRunning `tests/sdk-e2e/src/suites/tee-republish.test.ts` (the real relay→TEE→DB IPNS\nlease-renew round-trip) needs ALL of this wired consistently, or it fails in confusing ways.\nLearned the hard way in Phase 67 (2026-07-01).\n\n**DB is `cipherbox`, not `cipherbox_test`.** `apps/api/.env` may carry a stray\n`DB_DATABASE=cipherbox_test` (a CI-only name), but the running API defaults to `cipherbox`\n(`app.module.ts` `configService.get('DB_DATABASE','cipherbox')`). Two docker postgres exist:\n`cipherbox-postgres` (host **5432**) and `irl-network-postgres` (host 5433). Force\n`DB_DATABASE=cipherbox` for the migration, the API, AND the e2e (the suite defaults to\n`cipherbox` but pass it explicitly). The TypeORM migration CLI (`data-source.ts`) reads `.env`,\nso `pnpm migration:run` alone hits the stray `cipherbox_test` — override:\n`DB_DATABASE=cipherbox pnpm migration:run`.\n\n**Rebuild the tee-worker; it is usually weeks-stale.** `docker compose ps` may not even list it\n(the running container often predates the compose `tee-worker` service). Always\n`docker compose -f docker/docker-compose.yml up -d --build tee-worker`. It binds host **3002**\n→ container 3001 (host 3001 is `mock-ipns-routing`). Simulator TEE key is DETERMINISTIC (HKDF\nfrom fixed seed `cipherbox-tee-simulator-seed`), so a rebuild keeps the same key and existing\n`tee_key_state` stays valid.\n\n**Restart the API from CURRENT source.** It runs as `node dist/main` (NOT nest watch), so a\nrunning instance serves stale compiled code — `pnpm --filter @cipherbox/api build`, kill the\n:3000 node, then start with the full env:\n`DB_DATABASE=cipherbox TEE_WORKER_URL=http://localhost:3002 TEE_WORKER_SECRET=dev-secret TEST_LOGIN_SECRET=[REDACTED] node dist/main`.\nInline env wins over `.env` (dotenv no-override). On startup `TeeService.initializeFromTee()`\npopulates `tee_key_state` from the worker (only when the worker is healthy + the bearer secret\nmatches — `/republish` and `/public-key` are behind `TEE_WORKER_SECRET` auth; `/health` is open).\n\n**Secrets must match on both sides.** `TEST_LOGIN_SECRET` (API) must equal the harness default\n`e2e-test-secret-do-not-use-in-production` (or set `SDK_E2E_SECRET` to the API's value) or every\naccount create 401s. `TEE_WORKER_SECRET` API↔worker must match (`dev-secret`).\n\n**The suite reads `tee_key_state.current_public_key` (bytea) + `current_epoch`** — there is NO\n`public_key` column; return the bytea as hex for `createSubfolder`'s `hexToBytes`. Redis is\n**6380**. Determinism: direct-pg `UPDATE ... next_republish_at = NOW() - interval` + ONE\n`queue.add('republish-batch', {})` on the `republish` queue — no cron/timer wait.\n\nSee [[project-sdk-e2e-only-cross-package-publish-gate]] (the general gate + secret trap) and\n[[project-staging-vps-access]].\n"
}
```

id: toolu_01VTDe86ijAqdKF5uryHALp7
```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/myankelev/.claude/projects/-Users-myankelev-Code-random-cipher-box/memory/project-grep-ac-forces-runtime-broken-impl.md",
  "content": "---\nname: project-grep-ac-forces-runtime-broken-impl\ndescription: Grep-based plan acceptance criteria can push an implementation that passes static+mock checks but is broken at runtime; the sdk-e2e round-trip is what catches it\nmetadata:\n  node_type: memory\n  type: feedback\n---\n\nA GSD plan acceptance criterion that greps for a specific construct can force an executor to\nchoose an implementation shape purely to satisfy the grep — even when a simpler shape is more\ncorrect. Phase 67 (2026-07-01): plan 67-07 required `grep -nE \"innerJoin|tombstoned_at IS NULL\"\nin getDueEntries`, so the executor wrote a TypeORM query-builder `innerJoin(IpnsRecord,'r',...)`\n+ raw-column `orderBy('s.next_republish_at')` + `take(2000)`. That path throws\n`Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'databaseName')` at RUNTIME (TypeORM can't resolve\nthe raw snake_case column into entity metadata during take-pagination) — so `processRepublishBatch`\nnever reached the TEE. The unit spec MOCKED `createQueryBuilder`, so it passed; the grep passed;\n`tsc` passed. Only the live `sdk-e2e` round-trip surfaced it. Fix: the pre-existing\n`find`-options query (property names) + a paired `ipns_records.find` — same guarantee, robust.\nThe executor's own 67-07 SUMMARY even listed \"two-step QB ... satisfying innerJoin grep\nrequirement\" as a decision — a tell that the AC drove the shape.\n\n**Why:** greps and mock-based unit tests both check *form/shape*, not runtime behavior against a\nreal DB/ORM. TypeORM query-builder + raw column + `take` is a known footgun; prefer repository\n`find`-options (property names) for select/order/paginate.\n\n**How to apply:** (1) treat grep-only ACs with suspicion — verify the construct actually RUNS,\nnot just that it's present. (2) For any TypeORM select with order+take, use `find({where,order,\ntake})` with property names, not `createQueryBuilder().orderBy('alias.snake_col').take()`. (3) A\nmocked-query-builder unit test proves nothing about the real query — the sdk-e2e round-trip is\nthe authoritative gate for relay/DB query changes. See\n[[project-sdk-e2e-only-cross-package-publish-gate]] and [[project-tee-republish-e2e-stack-recipe]].\n"
}
```

> AGENT

The script exists in `apps/api`. Run it with the filter (or from that dir):

```bash
DB_DATABASE=cipherbox pnpm --filter @cipherbox/api migration:run
```

`data-source.ts` reads `.env`, and inline env wins over dotenv — so the `DB_DATABASE=cipherbox` prefix overrides the stray `cipherbox_test` in `apps/api/.env` and targets the right DB.
